\frac{M}{p}\bar{\Delta}^{2}=\frac{M}{p}\bar{\Delta}^{4}\frac{1}{\bar{\Delta}^{2}}\leq\frac{c_{2}^{2}\log(KM)}{c_{1}\log(KM)}\leq 1\kern 5.0pt,

provide that we chose
c_{2}\leq\sqrt{c_{1}}
. Thus, there exists a numerical constant
c
such that for all
\pi\in V
, we have

KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})\leq cc_{2}^{2}\log(Km)\kern 5.0pt.

H.6.4
Proof of Lemma
H.4

Let us suppose that there exists a probability distribution
\rho
on
\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}
and
a>0
such that

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:R^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)-\rho(\left(\mathbb{R}^{p}\right)^{K}\setminus\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}})\geq a\kern 5.0pt.

Given an estimator
\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
, the previous hypothesis directly implies that there exists
\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
such that
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)-\rho(\left(\mathbb{R}^{p}\right)^{K}\setminus\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}})\geq a.

By definition,
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)=\int\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)d\rho(\mu).
The quantity
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)
being bounded by
1
, we have
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)\leq\int_{\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)d\rho(\mu)+\rho((\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}\setminus\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}).
Therefore,
\int_{\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)d\rho(\mu)\geq a.
This implies the existence of
\mu\in\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}
such that
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(\hat{\pi}\neq\pi)\geq a
. This being true for all estimator
\hat{\pi}
, we get the following inequality that concludes the Lemma

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\mu\in\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\neq 0)\geq a\kern 5.0pt.

H.7
Proof of Theorem
A.2
; Partial Recovery

In this section, we prove the second point of Theorem
A.2
. Let us suppose that
p\geq c\log(K)
and
K\geq K_{0}
, with
c
and
K_{0}
two numerical constants that we will choose large enough later. Without loss of generality, we suppose throughout this proof that
\sigma=1
.

To prove our result, we will apply the following lemma that we prove in Section
H.11
together with a reduction lemma.

Lemma H.6
.

Suppose that
K\geq K_{0}
where
K_{0}
is a numerical constant large enough. There exists
V
a set of
M
-tuple of permutations such that the following properties hold.

•

For all
\pi\in V
and
M\leq\lceil\frac{M}{2}\rceil
,
\pi_{m}=id
,

•

There exists a numerical constant
a
such that for any two different
M
-tuple of permutations
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
in
V
,
\frac{1}{MK}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{1}_{\pi^{\prime}_{m}(k)\neq\pi_{m}(k)}\geq a
,

•

There exists a numerical constant
c
such that
\log(|S|)\geq cMK\log(K)
.

In the following, we suppose that
K_{0}\geq 4
and we recall that we consider
K\geq K_{0}
. Here is the reduction lemma that we will apply in this proof. This lemma plays the same role in this proof as does Lemma
H.4
in Section
H.6
.

Lemma H.7
.

Suppose that there exists a probability distribution
\rho
on
(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}
and
C>0
satisfying

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]-\rho((\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}\setminus\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}})\geq C\kern 5.0pt.

Then, we have

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\mu\in\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq C\kern 5.0pt.

We will also need the following lemma which is a consequence of Fano’s lemma. We refer to Section
H.13
for its proof.

Lemma H.8
.

Let
\rho
be a probability distribution on
(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}
. For any finite set
A
of
(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
, we have the following inequality

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\frac{2}{|A|}\sum_{\pi\in A}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq\min_{\pi\neq\pi^{\prime}\in A}err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\left(1-\frac{1+\frac{1}{|A|}\sup_{\pi\in A}KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})}{\log(|A|)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

As in Section
H.6
, we distinguish two cases. In the first one, we suppose that there exists a constant
c_{1}
that we will choose small enough such that
\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{1}\log(K)
. In the second one, we will suppose that
c_{1}\log(K)\leq\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{M}\log(K)}
, for
c_{2}
a numerical constant that we will also choose small enough.

H.8
Case
\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{1}\log(K)

In this section, we suppose that
\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{1}\log(K)
. We suppose that the constant
c
such that
p\geq c\log(K)
is large enough for having
\frac{p}{4}\log(2)\geq\log(K)
. Our choice of the centers
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{K}
relies on the following lemma, proved in Section
H.14
.

Lemma H.9
.

If
\frac{p}{4}\log(2)\geq\log(K)
, there exists
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{K}
in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
such that
\frac{1}{2}\min_{k\neq l}\|\mu_{k}-\mu_{l}\|^{2}\geq\bar{\Delta}^{2}
and
\frac{1}{2}\max_{k\neq l}\|\mu_{k}-\mu_{l}\|^{2}\leq 4\bar{\Delta}^{2}
.

We consider
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{K})
in
(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}
that satisfies the conditions of this lemma. Given
\pi
taken in the set
V
defined in Lemma
H.6
, let us compute
KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,id})
. We denote
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi;k,m}
the marginal distribution of
Y_{k}^{(m)}
under the joint law
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}
. By independance of all the features, we have that

KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,id})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{m=1}^{M}KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi;k,m},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,id;k,m})\kern 5.0pt.

Given
k\in[K]
and
m\in[M]
,
KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi;k,m},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,id;k,m})=\frac{\|\mu_{k}-\mu_{\pi_{m}(k)}\|^{2}}{2}
. Hence, we have

KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,id})\leq 4KM\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq 4c_{1}KM\log(K)\kern 5.0pt.

Combining this with Lemma
H.8
and Lemma
H.6
leads us to

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\frac{2}{|V|}\sum_{\pi\in V}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq a\left(1-\frac{1+2c_{1}^{2}KM\log(K)}{cKM\log(K)}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

where
a
and
c
are positive numerical constants. The quantity
a\left(1-\frac{1+2c_{1}^{2}KM\log(K)}{cM\log(K)}\right)
being larger than
\frac{a}{2}
if
c_{1}
is small enough and
K_{0}
large enough, we conclude that there exists a constant
C
such that

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\mu\in\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}(err(\hat{\pi},\pi))\geq C\kern 5.0pt.

H.9
Case
c_{1}\log(K)\leq\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{M}\log(K)}

In this section, we suppose that
c_{1}\log(K)\leq\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{M}\log(K)}
with
c_{2}
a numerical constant that we will suppose small enough compared
c_{1}
–
c_{1}
now being considered as fixed. We still suppose that
K\geq K_{0}
, for
K_{0}
a large enough numerical constant. As in Section
H.6
, we denote
\rho
the uniform distribution on
\mathcal{E}=\{\varepsilon,-\varepsilon\}^{pK}
, where
\varepsilon=2\sqrt{\frac{1}{p}}\bar{\Delta}
.

Lemma H.10
.

We suppose that
c_{2}
is small with respect to
c_{1}
and that
c_{1}\log(K)\leq\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{M}\log(K)}
. Then, there exists a numerical constant
c>0
such that, for all
\pi\in V
, we have

KL\left(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id}\right)\leq cc_{2}^{2}KM\log(K)

We refer to Section
H.10
for a proof of this lemma. Combining Lemma
H.10
and Lemma
H.8
implies that

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\frac{2}{|V|}\sum_{\pi\in V}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq\min_{\pi\neq\pi^{\prime}\in V}err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\left(1-\frac{1+cc_{2}^{2}KM\log(K)}{c^{\prime}Km\log(K)}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

where
c
and
c^{\prime}
are two numerical constants. Given
\pi\neq\pi^{\prime}\in V
, for
m\in[1,\lceil\frac{M}{2}\rceil]
,
\pi_{m}=\pi^{\prime}_{m}
. Let
\psi
be a permutation of
[K]
. Then, for
k\in[K]
, if
\psi(k)\neq k
, we get
\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbf{1}_{\psi(\pi_{m}(k))\neq\pi^{\prime}_{m}(k)}\geq\lceil M/2\rceil\geq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{m}(k)\neq\pi^{\prime}_{m}(k)}
. Hence, from the definition (
6
), we have
err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=\frac{1}{MK}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{j}(i)\neq\pi^{\prime}_{j}(i)}
. From the definition of
V
in Lemma
H.6
, we deduce that
err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\geq a
, where
a
is a numerical constant. Hence,

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\frac{2}{|V|}\sum_{\pi\in V}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq a\left(1-\frac{1+cc_{2}^{2}Km\log(K)}{c^{\prime}KM\log(K)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

This last quantity is larger than
\frac{a}{2}
provided
c_{2}
is small enough and
K_{0}
large enough. We can conclude using Lemma
H.7
together with the fact that
\rho(\left(\mathbb{R}^{p}\right)^{K}\setminus\theta_{\Delta})\leq\frac{K(K-1)}{2}e^{\frac{-p}{8}}\leq\frac{a}{4}
provided the constant
c
such that
p\geq c\log(K)
is large enough. We refer to Lemma 14 of
[
CD16
]
for the inequality
\rho(\mathbb{R}^{p}\setminus\theta_{\Delta})\leq\frac{K(K-1)}{2}e^{\frac{-p}{8}}
.

H.10
Proof of Lemma
H.10

For
\pi\in V
, let us compute
KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})
. We recall that
KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})=\int\log\left(\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,G}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\overline{G}}}\right)d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,G}
. We write
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}
, or equivalently
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,id}
, the distribution of
Y\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times[K]\times[M]}
when
\mu_{1}=\ldots=\mu_{K}=0
almost surely. Under this distribution, the entries of
Y
are drawn independently according
\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{p}\right)
. First, we will compute the quantity

\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id}}=\frac{\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}}}{\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}}}=\frac{\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}}}{\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,id}}}\kern 5.0pt,

(69)

where the second equality comes from the fact that
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,id}
. Given a probability distribution
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}
on some Euclidean space, which is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure, we write
d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}
for the density of this distribution with respect to the Lebesgue measure.
For the numerator in (
69
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}}(Y)=

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(Y)\right]}{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\left[d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu,\pi}(Y)\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\prod_{k\in[K]}\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|Y_{k}^{(m)}-\mu_{\pi_{m}(k)}\|^{2}\right)\right]}{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{0}\left[\prod_{k\in[K]}\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|Y_{k}^{(m)}-\mu_{\pi_{m}(k)}\|^{2}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(Y_{k,d}^{(m)}-\mu_{\pi_{m}(k),d}\right)^{2}\right)\right]}{\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(Y_{k,d}^{(m)}\right)^{2}\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

Using the independence of the
\mu_{k,d}
’s under the law
\rho
, we get that

\displaystyle\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,\pi}}(Y)=

\displaystyle\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(Y_{\pi^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}-\mu_{k,d}\right)^{2}\right)\right]}{\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(Y_{\pi^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}\right)^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp{\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left((Y_{\pi^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}-\mu_{k,d})^{2}-(Y_{\pi^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)})^{2}\right)\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\prod_{m\in[M]}\exp{\left(Y_{\pi^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}\mu_{k,d}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}e^{\frac{-M\varepsilon^{2}}{2}}\cosh{\left(\sum_{m\in[M]}\varepsilon Y_{\pi^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

Similarly, we have

\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{0,id}}(Y)=\prod_{d\in[p]}\prod_{k\in[K]}e^{\frac{-M\varepsilon^{2}}{2}}\cosh{\left(\sum_{m\in[M]}\varepsilon Y_{k,d}^{(m)}\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

Combining these two equalities in (
69
), we end up with

\frac{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}}{d\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id}}(Y)=\prod_{d=1}^{p}\prod_{k=1}^{K}\frac{\cosh{\left(\sum_{m\in[M]}\varepsilon Y_{\pi_{m}^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}\right)}}{\cosh{\left(\sum_{m\in[M]}\varepsilon Y_{k,d}^{(m)}\right)}}\kern 5.0pt.

(70)

We denote
\phi
the standard Gaussian density
\phi(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{\frac{-x^{2}}{2}}
. Under the law
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}
, conditionally on
\mu_{1},\ldots\mu_{K}\sim\rho
, we have that:

•

\sum_{m\in[M]}\varepsilon Y_{\pi_{m}^{-1}(k),d}^{(m)}\sim\mathcal{N}(M\mu_{k,d},M)
,

•

\sum_{m\in[M]}\varepsilon Y_{k,d}^{(m)}\sim\mathcal{N}(\sum_{m\in[M]}\mu_{\pi_{m}(k),d},M)
.

Plugging these two points, together with equality (
70
), in the definition of the Kullback-Leibler divergence leads to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int\log\cosh(\varepsilon(M\mu_{k,1}+\sqrt{M}x))\phi(x)dx\right]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int\log\cosh(\varepsilon(\sum_{m\in[M]}\mu_{\pi_{m}(k),1}+\sqrt{M}x))\phi(x)dx\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(71)

By symmetry, it is sufficient to upper-bound the term corresponding to
k=1
in the sum above. We call
S_{1}=S_{11}-S_{12}
the corresponding terms with

S_{1}=S_{11}-S_{12}
, with

\displaystyle S_{11}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int\log\cosh(\varepsilon(M\mu_{1,1}+\sqrt{M}x))\phi(x)dx\right],

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad S_{12}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int\log\cosh(\varepsilon(\sum_{m\in[M]}\mu_{\pi_{m}(1),1}+\sqrt{M}x))\phi(x)dx\right].

First, we upper-bound the term
S_{11}
. We will use the following inequality

\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{x^{4}}{12}\leq\log\cosh(x)\leq\frac{x^{2}}{2},\kern 5.0pt\forall x\in\mathbb{R}\kern 5.0pt.

(72)

Proof of inequality (
72
).

Let us first prove the upper-bound
\log\cosh(x)\leq\frac{x^{2}}{2}
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
\cosh(x)=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{x^{2t}}{(2t)!}\leq\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{x^{2t}}{t!2^{t}}=\exp\left(\frac{t^{2}}{2}\right)
. Applying the logarithmic function leads us to
\log\cosh(x)\leq\frac{x^{2}}{2}
.

Let us now prove the lower-bound
\log\cosh(x)\geq\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{x^{4}}{12}
. To do so, we write, for
x\geq 0
,
f(x)=\tanh(x)
and
g(x)=x-\frac{x^{3}}{3}
. We have
f^{\prime}(x)=1-(f(x))^{2}
. Besides,
g^{\prime}(x)=1-x^{2}\leq 1-(g(x))^{2}
. Together with the fact that
f(0)=g(0)=0
, this implies

f(x)\geq g(x),\kern 5.0pt\forall x\geq 0\kern 5.0pt.

Integrating these function leads to

\log\cosh(x)\geq\frac{x^{2}}{2}-\frac{x^{4}}{12},\kern 5.0pt\forall x\geq 0\kern 5.0pt.

By parity of these functions, this last inequality is satisfied for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
.
∎

Inequality (
72
), together with the independence of
x
and
\mu_{1,1}
, imply that

S_{11}\leq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int(\varepsilon(M\mu_{1,1}+\sqrt{M}x))^{2}\phi(x)dx\right]\leq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\left(M+M^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(73)

We arrive at

S_{11}\leq\frac{1}{2}M\varepsilon^{2}(1+M\varepsilon^{2})\kern 5.0pt.

(74)

Let us now lower-bound
S_{12}
. For
k\in[K]
, let us write
m_{k}=|\left\{m\in[M],\pi_{m}(1)=k\right\}|
. Inequality (
72
) induces

\displaystyle S_{12}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int\varepsilon^{2}\bigg(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}\mu_{k,1}+\sqrt{M}x\bigg)^{2}\phi(x)dx\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\int\varepsilon^{4}\bigg(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}\mu_{k,1}+\sqrt{M}x\bigg)^{4}\phi(x)dx\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}+M\right)-\frac{1}{12}\varepsilon^{4}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}\left[\bigg(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}\mu_{k,1}\bigg)^{4}\right]-\frac{1}{12}\varepsilon^{4}3M^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{4}M\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho}[(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}\mu_{k,1})^{2}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}+M\right)-\frac{1}{12}\varepsilon^{8}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{4}+6\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}\right)^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{4}M^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{6}M\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

We end up with

S_{12}\geq\frac{1}{2}M\varepsilon^{2}-\frac{1}{4}M^{2}\varepsilon^{4}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{6}M\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}-\frac{1}{12}\varepsilon^{8}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{4}+6\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}\right)^{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(75)

Combining inequalities (
74
) and (
75
), together with
M=\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}
, we get

\displaystyle S_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M\varepsilon^{2}(1+M\varepsilon^{2})-\frac{1}{2}M\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{1}{4}M^{2}\varepsilon^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{6}M\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}+\frac{1}{12}\varepsilon^{8}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{4}+6\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}m_{k}^{2}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}M^{2}\varepsilon^{4}+\frac{7}{12}\varepsilon^{8}M^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{6}M^{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cM^{2}\varepsilon^{4}\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}M+\varepsilon^{4}M^{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

where
c
is a numerical constant. Summing over
k\in[K]
in (
71
) leads to

KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})\leq cpKM^{2}\varepsilon^{4}\left(1+\varepsilon^{2}M+\varepsilon^{4}M^{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

From the definition of
\varepsilon
, we deduce that
pM^{2}K\varepsilon^{4}=\frac{16M^{2}K}{p}\bar{\Delta}^{4}
. The hypothesis
\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{M}\log(K)}
leads to
pMK\varepsilon^{4}\leq 16c_{2}^{2}MK\log(K)
. Moreover, the hypothesis
c_{1}\log(K)\leq\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{M}\log(K)}
implies
\varepsilon^{2}M=\frac{4M}{p}\frac{\bar{\Delta}^{4}}{\bar{\Delta}^{2}}\leq 4\frac{c_{2}^{2}}{c_{1}}\leq 1
provided that
c_{2}
is chosen small enough so that
c_{2}\leq\sqrt{c_{1}}
. Thus, there exists a numerical constant
c>0
such that, when
c_{2}
is small enough with respect to
c_{1}
,

KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})\leq cc_{2}^{2}MK\log(K)\kern 5.0pt.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

H.11
Proof of Lemma
H.6

To prove Lemma
H.6
, we will use Lemma 16 from
[
CD16
]
that we recall here.

Lemma H.11
.

For any
K\geq 4
. There exists
\psi^{(1)},\ldots,\psi^{(\ell)}\in\mathcal{S}_{K}
such that the following points hold:

1.

\ell\geq(\frac{K}{24})^{\frac{K}{6}}
,

2.

For any
t\neq t^{\prime}\in[\ell]
,
\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathbf{1}_{\psi^{(t)}(k)\neq\psi^{(t^{\prime})}(k)}\geq\frac{3}{8}
.

If
M\leq 3
, we simply define
V
of size
\ell
made of
\pi
’s of the form
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=id
and
\pi_{3}=\psi^{(l)}
for some
l=1,\ldots,\ell
. Obviously, this set
V
satisfies the desired properties of Lemma
H.6
.

Let us now consider the general case where
M\geq 4
. We construct
V
as follows. Denote
\overline{V}
the subset of
(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
made from the
M
-tuples
\pi
satisfying the two conditions;

1.

if
m\leq\lceil\frac{M}{2}\rceil
,
\pi_{m}=id
;

2.

if
m>\lceil\frac{M}{2}\rceil
,
\pi_{m}
is one of the
\psi^{(1)},\ldots,\psi^{(\ell)}
.

We take for
V
a maximal family in
\overline{V}
such that for
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
in
V
, there are at least
\frac{M}{4}
index such that
\pi_{m}\neq\pi^{\prime}_{m}
. Since for
t\neq t^{\prime}\in[\ell]
,
\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathbf{1}_{\psi^{(t)}(k)\neq\psi^{(t^{\prime})}(k)}\geq\frac{3}{8}
, we have that, for
\pi\neq\pi^{\prime}\in V
,
\frac{1}{KM}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{m\in[M]}\mathbf{1}\left\{\pi_{m}(k)\neq\pi^{\prime}_{m}(k)\right\}\geq\frac{3}{32}.

It remains to lower-bound the cardinality of
V
. First, we know that
|\overline{V}|=\ell^{\lfloor\frac{M}{2}\rfloor}
. For
\pi\in V
, denote by
B_{\pi}
the subset of
\overline{V}
made from all the
M
-tuple
\pi^{\prime}\in|\overline{V}|
that have less than
\frac{M}{4}
indices such that
\pi_{m}\neq\pi^{\prime}_{m}
. The definition of
V
implies that
\overline{V}\subset\bigcup_{\pi\in V}B_{\pi}
. A fortiori, since the cardinality of
B_{\pi}
is constant over
V
, we have
\ell^{\lfloor\frac{M}{2}\rfloor}\leq|V||B_{\pi}|
for any
\pi\in V
.

Let us upper-bound
|B_{\pi}|
for some
\pi\in V
. Any
\pi^{\prime}\in B_{\pi}
has at least
\frac{M}{4}
indice such that
\pi_{m}=\pi^{\prime}_{m}
among the indices
m>\lceil\frac{M}{2}\rceil
. Fixing
\lceil\frac{M}{4}\rceil
indices where the permutations are equal, there are at most
\ell^{\frac{M}{4}}
possibilities for choosing an element in
\overline{V}
that is equal to
\pi^{\prime}
on these index. Thus, we get that
|B_{\pi}|\leq\binom{\lfloor\frac{M}{2}\rfloor}{\lceil\frac{M}{4}\rceil}\ell^{\frac{M}{4}}
. Since
\binom{x}{y}\leq(xe/y)^{y}
.
We end up with

\displaystyle\log|V|\geq

\displaystyle\log(\ell^{\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\rfloor})-\log|B_{\pi}|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\lfloor\frac{M}{2}\rfloor\log(\ell)-\frac{M}{4}\log(\ell)-\lceil\frac{M}{4}\rceil\log(e\frac{\lfloor\frac{M}{2}\rfloor}{\lceil\frac{M}{4}\rceil})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{KM}{18}\log(\frac{K}{24})-\frac{KM}{24}\log(\frac{K}{24})-\lceil\frac{M}{4}\rceil\log(\frac{e\lfloor\frac{M}{2}\rfloor}{\lceil\frac{M}{4}\rceil})\kern 5.0pt.

When
K_{0}
is large enough, these inequalities imply
\log|V|\gtrsim MK\log(K)
whenever
K\geq K_{0}
. This concludes the proof of the Lemma.

H.12
Proof of Lemma
H.7

We suppose that there exists a probability distribution
\rho
on
(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}
and
C>0
satisfying

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]-\rho((\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}\setminus\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}})\geq C\kern 5.0pt.

Given
\hat{\pi}:(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{KM}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
, there exists a
M
-tuple of permutation
\pi
such that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi}\neq\pi)\right]-\rho((\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}\setminus\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}})\geq C
. Since
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{p})^{K}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]d\rho(\mu)
and
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]
is upper bounded by
1
, we end up with
\int_{\Theta_{\Delta}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]d\rho(\mu)\geq C
.

This implies the existence of
\mu\in\Theta_{\Delta}
such that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq C
. A fortiori,

\sup_{\mu\in\Theta_{\Delta}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq C\kern 5.0pt.

This last inequality being true for all estimator
\hat{\pi}
, we get the desired result

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\sup_{\mu\in\Theta_{\bar{\Delta}}}\sup_{\pi\in(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq C\kern 5.0pt.

H.13
Proof of Lemma
H.8

Let
A
be a subset of
(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
. Denote
\pi^{(1)},\ldots,\pi^{(|A|)}
the elements of
A
. Given any estimator
\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}
, we denote
\hat{j}
an index that minimises
err(\hat{\pi},\pi^{(j)})
.

For any
j\in[1,|A|]
, using the definition of
\hat{j}
together with the fact that the function
err
satisfies the triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle min_{j\neq j^{\prime}}err(\pi^{(j)},\pi^{(j^{\prime})})\mathbf{1}_{\hat{j}\neq j}

\displaystyle\leq err(\pi^{(\hat{j})},\pi^{(j)})

\displaystyle\leq err(\hat{\pi},\pi^{(\hat{j})})+err(\hat{\pi},\pi^{(j)})

\displaystyle\leq 2err(\hat{\pi},\pi^{(j)})\kern 5.0pt.

Taking the expectation over this last inequality, we get, for
j\in[1,|A|]
,

2\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi^{(j)}}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi^{(j)})\right]\geq min_{\pi\neq\pi^{\prime}\in A}err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi^{(j)}}\left[\hat{j}\neq j\right]\kern 5.0pt.

Summing over
\pi\in A
leads to

\frac{2}{|A|}\sum_{\pi\in A}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq\min_{\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in A}err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\min_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to A}\frac{1}{|A|}\sum_{\pi\in A}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[\hat{\pi}\neq\pi\right].

Applying Lemma
H.3
, we arrive at the desired conclusion

\inf_{\hat{\pi}:\mathbb{R}^{K\times M\times p}\to(\mathcal{S}_{K})^{M}}\frac{2}{|A|}\sum_{\pi\in A}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\rho,\pi}\left[err(\hat{\pi},\pi)\right]\geq\min_{\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in A}err(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\left(1-\frac{1+\frac{1}{|A|}\sum_{\pi\in A}KL(\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,\pi},\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\rho,id})}{\log(|A|)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

H.14
Proof of Lemma
H.9

We suppose that
\frac{p}{4}\log(2)\geq\log(K)
and we want to find vectors
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{K}
in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
such that
\frac{1}{2}\min_{k\neq l}\|\mu_{k}-\mu_{l}\|^{2}\geq\bar{\Delta}^{2}
and
\frac{1}{2}\max_{k\neq l}\|\mu_{k}-\mu_{l}\|\leq 4\bar{\Delta}^{2}.
Denote
\mathcal{H}=\bar{\Delta}\sqrt{\frac{2}{p}}\{-1,1\}^{p}
. There are
2^{p}
elements in
\mathcal{H}
. Consider
H
a maximal subset of
\mathcal{H}
such that the following holds. For
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
two distinct elements of
H
, there exist at least
p/4
indices such that
\mu_{i}\neq\mu^{\prime}_{i}
.

If
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
are two distinct elements of
H
, we have from the definitions of
\mathcal{H}
and
H
that

\bar{\Delta}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\mu-\mu^{\prime}\|^{2}\leq 4\bar{\Delta}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

It remains to lower-bound the cardinality of
H
and to prove
|H|\geq K
. Given
\mu\in H
, denote
B_{\mu}
the subset of
\mathcal{H}
made of the elements
\mu^{\prime}
that have at least
\lfloor\frac{3p}{4}\rfloor
indices such that
\mu_{i}=\mu^{\prime}_{i}
. From the definition of
H
, we deduce that
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mu\in H}B_{\mu}
.

Let us upper-bound
|B_{\mu}|
for any
\mu\in H
. Given a fixed set of index of cardinal
\lfloor\frac{3p}{4}\rfloor
, there are at most
2^{\frac{p}{4}}
points in
\mathcal{H}
that are equal to
\mu
on these indices. Hence, the cardinal of
B_{\mu}
is upper-bounded by
\binom{p}{\lfloor\frac{3p}{4}\rfloor}2^{\frac{p}{4}}
.

Combining this with
\mathcal{H}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mu\in H}B_{\mu}
, we arrive at
\log(|H|)\geq p\log(2)-\log\binom{p}{\lfloor\frac{3p}{4}\rfloor}-\frac{p}{4}\log(2)
. We have

\log\binom{p}{\lfloor\frac{3p}{4}\rfloor}\leq\lceil\frac{p}{4}\rceil\log(ep/\lfloor\frac{3p}{4}\rfloor)\leq\frac{p}{2}\log(2)\ ,

since
p\log(2)/4\geq\log(K)\geq\log(K_{0})
is large enough. Thus, we end up with
\log(|H|)\geq\frac{p}{4}\log(2)\geq\log(K)
. This concludes the proof.
